Major developers, legal experts, and 32 states back Epic in Apple’s SCOTUS appeal

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a significant development in Apple’s Supreme Court appeal of a civil contempt ruling, Epic Games has received backing from 32 states and the District of Columbia, along with major developers, legal experts, former federal judges, and more. Here are the details.

Apple’s ever-sprawling fight against Epic Games over its App Store policies is currently being fought on two fronts: in the Supreme Court, where Apple is seeking to overturn a civil contempt ruling, and in the district court, where the companies are disputing what commission, if any, Apple can charge on purchases made through external payment systems.

The civil contempt ruling came after Judge Yvonne Gonzalez Rogers found that Apple had violated an earlier injunction issued in its App Store dispute with Epic.

In a nutshell, after she ordered Apple to allow developers to direct users to alternative payment methods outside the App Store, the company responded by allowing external payment links, but imposed a commission of up to 27% on purchases made through them.

Since then, Apple has been trying to overturn the civil contempt ruling, basically arguing that the original injunction did not explicitly prohibit the commissions and other restrictions it imposed on external purchases.

After the Ninth Circuit upheld the contempt finding and denied its request for rehearing, Apple took the case to the Supreme Court, which agreed to hear its appeal.

Importantly, the Supreme Court will only consider the legal standard used to uphold Apple’s civil contempt ruling, not the separate question of what commission, if any, the company can charge on purchases made through external payment methods.

That will still be determined in the district court, where proceedings have continued despite Apple’s attempts to put them on hold pending the Supreme Court’s decision.

With oral arguments scheduled for January 11, 2027, the case has recently seen a flurry of activity, including Apple’s opening brief in September and Epic’s response earlier this month.

Apple also received support from several organizations in September, including the Washington Legal Foundation, the International Center for Law and Economics, the Computer & Communications Industry Association, and the U.S. Chamber of Commerce. The US government also submitted a brief supporting neither party, arguing that the contempt finding was unjustified over Apple’s 27% commission, but warranted over its restrictions on button designs.

Which brings us to the latest developments: a series of amicus briefs filed in support of Epic Games.

Last week, a total of 9 amicus briefs were filed in support of Epic Games, including one led by Utah and joined by 31 other states and the District of Columbia.

This particular brief notes that “the district court correctly held Apple in contempt for continuing to engage in anticompetitive conduct barred by a prior injunction,” adding:

“The court found that Apple engaged in unlawful anti-steering restrictions and required Apple to lift those restrictions. Apple then lifted them in name only, imposed new (and substantially similar) strictures on developers that mirrored the old ones and produced the same result. Worse still, Apple did all this in bad faith: Apple knew its behavior was wrong and actively hid that fact from the court, including by providing false testimony. Under any reasonable reading of the district court’s injunction, Apple’s conduct is worthy of civil contempt.”

The states also argue that “Injunctions in competition cases cannot afford complete relief without addressing the ongoing effects of anticompetitive behavior,” and that “[c]onstruing competition injunctions narrowly defeats their purpose and prevents meaningful redress.”

Similar arguments appear throughout the other briefs, which include submissions from Spotify, Match Group, and Microsoft, as well as nonprofit organizations such as the American Antitrust Institute (AAI), Public Knowledge, and the Electronic Frontier Foundation (EFF).

In their joint brief, Spotify, Match Group, and Microsoft argue that they “have first-hand experience with the effects of Apple’s conduct and evasion efforts,” adding that “Apple’s new restrictions were no different from the unlawful rules they replaced.”

As for the brief filed by former federal judges, they warned that “[i]n cases like these, where enjoined parties seek to circumvent an injunction through reliance on its literal text, courts must have the power of contempt to deter and remedy evasion. Otherwise, courts would be forced into a game of whack-a-mole, trying to anticipate every possible means of evading an injunction […]”

Arguments by the Little Tech Association, AAI, Public Knowledge, and the EFF all follow a similar line, with some also addressing how Apple’s restrictions on external payment links could affect consumer choice, pricing, and competition.

Comment | A missed opportunity for a full accounting of cultural cleansing

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An installation view of Heritage in Resistance: Timbuktu to Odessa, at the Cité de l’Architecture et du Patrimoine in Paris, until 3 January 2027 Photo: © Didier Plowy

It is always gratifying to see one’s work referenced—especially 20 years after the fact. The exhibition Heritage in Resistance: Timbuktu to Odessa, at the Cité de l’Architecture et du Patrimoine in Paris (until 3 January 2027), traces attacks on material and intangible culture in conflicts beginning with the dynamiting of the Bamiyan Buddhas in 2001 and ending in Ukraine, Sudan and Gaza.

The vast exhibition spans art, digital re-creations, photography and filmed interviews. It is conceived of as an investigative report organised into sections on erasure, resistance and repair.

A wall text references my 2006 book The Destruction of Memory: Architecture at War, which first set out systematically why such targeting happens. The exhibition credits me with coining the term “cultural cleansing” in respect of the Bosnian War to explain the pattern of heritage erasure that can accompany, or presage, ethnic cleansing and the persecution of populations. It notes that Unesco adopted the term from 2012 to describe “the destruction of World Heritage Sites by terrorist groups”.

Except that book says much more than that. It talked about this same process in other places such as the Israel-occupied West Bank. It examined cultural genocide (like cultural cleansing, this still has no definition in international law) witnessed in instances ranging from the Ottoman Empire and Native American populations to the Holocaust and Tibet. I would now add the experiences of the Uyghurs in China, the Rohingya in Myanmar, the Yazidis in Iraq and the Palestinians in Gaza to that dreadful list. I argue that patterns of heritage destruction can evidence genocidal intent. Other genocides, such as that in Rwanda, did not have a pronounced material culture component.

These are matters that the Cité de l’Architecture et du Patrimoine exhibition unacceptably skirts. As in Germany and the UK (where a talk at London’s Building Centre including Architects for Gaza was cancelled following an intervention by Lawyers for Israel this summer), the French state and its institutions are censoring pro-Palestinian voices.The crucial work of Forensic Architecture in documenting destruction, for example, is not present after a dispute over the group’s use of “the G word”. How can you explain what has happened to Gaza’s cultural heritage unless you are at least prepared to discuss possible intent and patterns of destruction?

As an investigative report, the show obfuscates as much as it reveals. Institutions and governments shield themselves behind the argument that it is up to the courts to determine genocide. They forget the Genocide Convention’s duties of prevention—as litigated in the 2007 International Court of Justice (ICJ) case, Bosnia and Herzegovina v. Serbia and Montenegro, or South Africa’s inclusion of attacks on culture when considering Palestinian “conditions of life” at the ICJ.

An installation view of Heritage in Resistance: Timbuktu to Odessa, at the Cité de l’Architecture et du Patrimoine in Paris, until 3 January 2027 Photo: © Didier Plowy

There is an impression that heritage destruction in conflicts has got worse over recent decades and that the propaganda impact of destruction is actually exploited by some groups. The jury is out on this. As reflected in the show, however, the international heritage community has had an almost singular focus on actions by terrorist groups, whereas over the past decades (Islamic State and its affiliates aside) it has been the firepower of United Nations State Parties that has been overwhelmingly responsible for the devastation to material history—including but not limited to the United States, Russia, China, Israel and Saudi Arabia. Isis iconoclastically wrecked religious sites in Mosul, but it was the liberating forces that comprehensively flattened the historic city.

Looting, too, is problematically covered in the Paris exhibition. This is a serious issue in war zones where security vanishes. But the myth repeated here that the sale of looted artefacts is a meaningful source of terrorist funding “on a par” with resources such as oil is simply unevidenced. (Recovered Isis documents show something quite different.)

The exhibition is sponsored in part by the French office of Unesco. No surprise then that the organisation’s manifest failure to protect heritage in conflicts is absent from the walls. Unesco is celebrated—as it should be in some cases—for its work in Timbuktu, where the International Criminal Court brought the first (and, to date, only) successful prosecution for the targeting of monuments as a war crime. Less convincingly, the show argues that this episode was a turning point after which Unesco formulated a more grassroots approach to preserving culture by working with local people on local priorities.

It was Unesco that disgracefully praised Putin for liberating Palmyra despite the terrible damage Russian forces allied with Assad inflicted on Syria’s people and heritage. Unesco has been just as partial since: while it “strongly condemns” Russian attacks on heritage in Ukraine and Israel’s on Lebanon, it does not offer the same degree of pressure in its statements on heritage destruction in Gaza or Iran. Even then, unlike other UN agencies, Unesco never names Israel. Unesco has not invoked the 1954 Hague Convention in relation to Gaza, despite citing it in virtually all similar instances. Unesco did not seek urgent action by the UN Security Council or the General Assembly to protect cultural sites there as it did in respect of Isis.

The Paris show is more subtle in its understanding of the ways that post-war heritage reconstruction can be problematic and not necessarily healing, but is just as silent on Unesco’s move away from the centrality of authenticity to reconstruction work. Whereas the notion of rebuilding the Bamiyan Buddhas was deemed unacceptable, Unesco promoted building a brand-new Mostar Bridge in Bosnia and Herzegovina as peacebuilding—despite community relations actually deteriorating in the city. And then the agency had the temerity to declare the facsimile bridge a World Heritage Site, undermining its own scientific tenets to bolster a failed symbolic gesture. All this bad-faith is set out in my follow-up book, Monumental Lies (2022).

One is left to read between the lines for hints at the full curatorial mindset. Once through the exhibition doors, the first large-scale works displayed are photographs by the Palestinian artist Taysir Batniji of Gazans’ retrieved housekeys, with their heartbreakingly quotidian fobs—a friendship bracelet, a cheap carabiner clip, a Minnie Mouse figure. The unknown fate of their owners is pencilled in below.

In its silences and voids, Heritage in Resistance at least accurately mirrors failures of international institutions and laws and the partiality and misinformation that surrounds responses to contemporary heritage destruction. Ultimately, the show offers false hope about successful resistance via digital surveying, reconstruction and Unesco work. What we should be feeling is despair.

Brief truce allowed International Council on Monuments and Sites and Palestinian antiquities ministry to investigate damage on the ground

The “situation” around the fourth-century Monastery of Saint Hilarion has "deteriorated rapidly" due to the war, an expert says

Amid Russia’s savagery we must maintain a clear ethical stance when intervening to protect culture

Anthropic Stock Is Down 33% From Its All-Time High. Here's What a $5,000 Investment Could Be Worth in 1 Year.

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic's post-IPO sell-off has been brutal, and history suggests an investment could produce dramatically different outcomes over the next year.

By Adam Spatacco – Nov 25, 2026 at 5:15PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

The artificial intelligence (AI) revolution has made 2026 a historic year for initial public offerings (IPOs). In May, chip designer Cerebras Systems (CBRS +1.01%) raised $6.4 billion through its IPO, followed by Elon Musk's Space Exploration Technologies' (SPCX +1.30%) record-setting $75 billion offering in June.

However, AI developer Anthropic (ANT +1.08%) subsequently eclipsed SpaceX's record when it made its Nasdaq debut on Nov. 19. Despite the initial enthusiasm surrounding the maker of Claude, Anthropic stock has already plunged 33% from its all-time high.

Let's explore Anthropic's growth prospects, assess its competitive landscape, and examine what history suggests a $5,000 investment could be worth around the company's first-year IPO anniversary.

Including the full exercise of the underwriters' option to purchase additional shares, Anthropic's IPO generated $93.5 billion in gross proceeds. Anthropic also completed a concurrent $26 billion private placement involving Nvidia, Microsoft, Alphabet, Advanced Micro Devices, Broadcom, Micron Technology, and Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing. Combined gross proceeds across the two transactions reached $119.5 billion.

When Anthropic began trading, shares opened around $111.50. The company's valuation reached approximately $2 trillion, instantly propelling Anthropic to one of the world's most valuable companies. Shares subsequently climbed to $115.10, although investors have since grown more cautious about the company over the last week.

Anthropic's revenue growth is nothing short of extraordinary. In 2023, the company generated just $41 million in sales. By 2025, the company's top-line had increased by more than 100x -- reaching $4.6 billion.

During the third quarter alone, Anthropic grew revenue by approximately 1,127% year over year to $17 billion. Furthermore, the company exited October with an annualized revenue run rate of $81 billion.

Despite Claude's historic adoption rate, Anthropic's infrastructure requirements and competitive landscape complicate the company's investment thesis.

Anthropic faces competition from two categories. The first stems from other dedicated frontier AI developers such as OpenAI, the maker of ChatGPT. The second source of competition includes diversified technology companies such as Alphabet and SpaceX, which develop Gemini and Grok, respectively.

Big tech conglomerates have alternative revenue streams that can help finance their AI ambitions. Alphabet operates high-margin advertising and cloud computing businesses, while SpaceX generates revenue from satellite connectivity and rocket launches.

These financial resources afford Anthropic's rivals greater flexibility when pricing their AI products. As large language models (LLMs) become increasingly sophisticated and open-weight alternatives mature, Anthropic may face pressure to reduce prices in order to retain customers.

Meanwhile, Anthropic has accumulated $901 billion in infrastructure commitments stretching across the next several years. During the third quarter alone, the company incurred $9.9 billion in computing and infrastructure expenses.

If rising competition drives AI pricing lower while capacity expenses remain elevated, Anthropic could struggle to translate its revenue growth into sustainable profits. This outcome could cause investors to re-rate the company with a lower price-to-sales (P/S) multiple, even as its revenue continues expanding.

To understand how Anthropic stock might perform over the next year, let's consider how several major technology IPOs performed during their first year as public companies.

Amazon and Alphabet generated first-year returns of 281% and 179%, respectively, while Tesla stock appreciated by 18%. By contrast, Meta Platforms, Uber, and Rivian experienced first-year declines of 31%, 21%, and 67%, respectively.

Although the average first-year return across these six companies washed out to about 60%, the results were heavily skewed by Amazon and Alphabet. The median return was actually negative, while the average and median maximum drawdowns during the first year were approximately 49% and 46%, respectively.

Perhaps more relevant to Anthropic is a separate analysis of the 10 largest U.S. IPOs between 2000 and 2024. Across this cohort, the average first-year return was -26%, while the median return was -34%. Furthermore, the average and median maximum drawdowns were 54% and 53%, respectively.

Using Anthropic's recent share price of $80, a decline consistent with the average or median first-year return among these mega-IPOs would imply a stock price between $53 and $59. Under these scenarios, a $5,000 investment would shrink to roughly $3,300 and $3,700.

Conversely, if Anthropic were to match the average first-year return of Alphabet or Amazon, shares could reach $223. This would transform a $5,000 investment into nearly $14,000.

Ultimately, history illustrates just how unpredictable investing in IPO stocks can be. While the performance of previous mega-IPOs suggests Anthropic could trade lower around its first anniversary, the limited number of successful offerings demonstrates that meaningful upside is also possible.

Given the range of possible outcomes, investors considering buying Anthropic stock might be better served by gradually building a position over the long term as Claude’s competitive strengths and the company’s long-term profitability become clearer.

Thanksgiving parade 2026: Macy’s Thanksgiving Parade start time, streaming options, weather concerns

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Updated on Nov. 25 at 4:40 p.m. ET — Updated to reflect that the parade has been postponed to Saturday, Nov. 27, due to weather.

Thanksgiving is almost here, and for some folks, the most important part of the holiday isn't the food, family, or football. It's watching a bunch of big floats during a parade in New York City.

OK, maybe that's not literally true, but the Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade is an annual touchstone for lots of folks, and it's almost here. As always, there are lots of things to know about it, such as when it starts, where you can watch it, and, this year especially, whether the weather will affect the parade as expected. Let's get into it.

First and foremost, the parade starts at 8:30 a.m. ET and runs until noon on Saturday. Yes, you read that right: The Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade is not actually on Thanksgiving Day this year. There are very good reasons for that, which we'll get to in a second.

It's worth noting that, unlike most other live televised events, it will not air simultaneously in every time zone. Instead, per NBC News, it will air from 8:30 to around noon in each time zone, so no matter where you are, turn the TV on at 8:30.

The parade will air on NBC, so if you have a TV that gets local channels via cable, an antenna, or another way to watch TV, just turn on NBC. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

If you don't have any of those things, Peacock will work, too. I should note that you'll need at least Peacock Premium to stream your local NBC station, and there is no free trial for that. You'll either have to pay $12.99 per month or borrow someone else's login.

According to NBC News, this year's parade will feature 100 inflatables in total, the most they've ever used for one of these. You can see the full balloon lineup on the Macy's website, but rest assured that all your favorites are likely included, like Sonic the Hedgehog and the Pink Panther. There are also new ones, like a DoorDash float and a GEICO float. One float will also act as a tribute of sorts for Fiona the hippo, which is nice.

Performers include Teddy Swims and Megan Moroney on the main stage, with other floats featuring acts such as the Baha Men, Blues Traveler, Icona Pop, and Devo. There's a little something for everyone.

And now for the bad news: There's a weather system moving up the East Coast that has caused the parade organizers to reschedule it this year, per NBC News.

Two storms combined into one in the American South, with the result expected to bring wind gusts of up to 45mph to New York City starting on Thursday morning. This is a problem because the city has an upper limit of 34mph for gusts regarding big inflatable objects. The parade was still scheduled for Thursday until the middle of Wednesday afternoon, when organizers and New York City officials decided to push it back to Saturday.

Unfortunately, that means you'll need a plan B for Thursday morning entertainment, but, more importantly, this should keep everyone who planned to attend the parade on Thursday safe.

Motorola Signature 27 hands-on: Pure elegance on display

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It’s hard to believe we finally got a proper premium phone from Motorola again, and it feels a bit like the return of the Moto X.

Motorola’s slow regeneration in North America from a mostly budget device-minded company to one offering the most expensive non-folding flagship phone might become the thing of legend. That’s not to say the company hasn’t shown true greatness in the past — the Moto X, Moto Z, and Razr lines are proof of that — but it’s always shied away from pulling out all the stops until the Motorola Signature 27 arrived.

Freshly unboxed and sitting in my hands, the textile-clad Capulet Olive Motorola Signature 27 colorway has impressed me in every way, shape, and form over the past 24 hours. It’s a much longer period of time than I usually get with hands-on opportunities and, yet, the newness of the experience has yet to wear off.

Part and parcel to that experience is the design. For whatever reason, this is the year Motorola seems to have truly figured out what makes a phone nice to hold. The textile back, ribbed metal frame, rounded corners, and curved edges on the front and back of the phone are purely blissful to hold. It’s a reminder that a phone can be as good looking as it feels instead of being a flat, boring, off-balance slab like too many phones are now.

The weight also surprised me. On paper this sits right in the middle of all the Ultra/Pro Max-named phones, yet it feels lighter for some intangible reason. The camera hump makes it slightly top-heavy, and it’s almost as wobble-free as a Pixel (there’s just one off-center camera hump).

The new 120Hz LTPO tandem OLED display is as top-notch as you’re going to get. I had all of maybe 2 hours of direct sunlight yesterday to test it with, and boy does it ever get bright. The rest of the time, I’ve been either inside or out in overcast weather, all of which it looked brilliant in. And it uses a combination of DC dimming above 20% brightness, and 2160Hz PWM dimming below 20% brightness, making it super comfortable for me.

Motorola’s software has evolved again with this release, and the verdict is still out on whether or not this is actually better than the company’s Android 16 designs. The build of Android 17 that ships with this phone utilizes some major design changes we’ve only seen on a few random low-end Motorola phones, and I’m not sure I care for the gentle gradients on the quick toggle icons and some other system UI elements. It feels like a weirdly cheap effect that clashes with Material Design’s flatter motif.

Despite that, this is the most Pixelly (is that a word?) a Motorola phone has ever felt. Features like Rambler, Sign to Text, and Create My Widget are here and work impeccably well. Gemini Intelligence is also on the way, and while there’s no specific ETA for that at the moment, it’s exciting to know that the most advanced Android features — which are currently only available on a very small number of devices — are coming to this phone.

This may also be the fastest phone I’ve used to date. Everything is unbelievably snappy. Tap an icon and the thing opens instantly. Animations are zippy, and the new lock screen and customization options feel cohesive. Plus, all the favorite Moto actions and gestures are still here, which I greatly appreciate.

I’ve got some lofty expectations for this camera hardware, too, and it’s because Motorola is talking up such a big game. DXOMark’s camera review rated it the 2nd-best smartphone camera in the world, just under the Huawei Pura 80 Ultra and above the iPhone 18 Pro. Some people don’t take much stock in DXOMark, but it’s still worth noting that they’ve praised the heck out of the camera and Motorola is also pushing this as a winner in a way they’ve never done before.

The hype for the Motorola Signature 27 certainly seems to be realized, and now only time will truly tell if it can live up to the lofty expectations Motorola’s $1,599 price has set for it. If nothing, it’s certainly off to a good start and has made an excellent first impression.

The Micron Crucial P510 is a PCIe 5.0 drive with 10,000 MB/s and 8,700 MB/s rated sequential read/write performance.

It's not the fastest PCIe 5.0 SSD, but it's still faster than the fastest PCIe 4.0 SSDs, as you can see from the data below that we gathered in our Cruicial P510 2TB review. Both sequential and random performance hit the marks established on the spec sheet, and in a relatively power efficient manner.

Perhaps what stood out most in our testing was the sustained performance of P510, offering a showcase of DRAM-less SSDs not falling apart when the cache is fully saturated.

The drive uses a Phison E31T controller and Micron 276-layer TLC flash. For the 2TB model, Micron rates endurance at 1,200 TBW, and the drive comes with a five-year warranty. SSDs are very expensive right now, but the Crucial P510 2TB for $294.99 is one of the better deals we've seen on a PCIe 5.0 drive.

Screens 5.10 delivered two highly-requested desktop features to users earlier this month. Now the app is back with a fun update for iPhone Duo users: tiny laptop mode.

The true box office winner of Dunesday has been predicted by the Nielsen ratings, and it's none other than Avengers: Doomsday.

Per Deadline, NRG (National Research Group, owned by Nielsen) predicts that Doomsday will bring in $270 million at the global box office during its opening weekend. Dune 3 is predicted to bring in $75 million.

This is the highest projection NRG has ever recorded, with Spider-Man: Brand New Day being the second-highest. To date, Avengers: Endgame now holds the record second-highest opening weekend for any movie ever at $357.1M, having been beat out by Spider-Man: Brand New Day's $360 million.

Avengers: Doomsday tickets went on sale back in July for the special Infinity Vision screenings. Wanda will indeed return in Doomsday, but she won't be, uh, herself... if you catch my drift.

Dune 3 tickets have been a bit of a nightmare for fans as they sold out almost immediately back in April, and then crashed several movie theater apps when more tickets were released back in August. Oh, and, when tickets did sell out in April... people started reselling them for up to $1000. The people yearn for sand.

Why Is Netflix’s Guarding Stars A Christmas Movie When Its Book Is Not? I Asked Jared Padalecki

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

I Just Realized Leighton Meester And Jared Padalecki's New Rom-Com Is Based On A Book I Loved

Jared Padalecki On Jumping From The Boys Into A Netflix Rom-Com: 'You Really Want To Take A Shower'

Jared Padalecki And Leighton Meester Bonded Over Gossip Girl And Gilmore Girls In A G-Oriented Way I Didn't See Coming

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

I had to ask the star of The Bodyguard's adaptation.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Katherine Center’s novel, The Bodyguard, is not about Christmas. Not even a little bit. So, when I learned its book-to-screen adaptation, Guarding Stars, was part of Netflix’s 2026 Christmas movie lineup, I was confused. So, I had to ask Jared Padalecki why they decided to involve the holiday in this film.

Notably, the novel, The Bodyguard, takes place in the fall, and Jack and Hannah’s whole experience in Texas centers around Thanksgiving. However, for the movie, the story got moved to Montana and pushed back a month, meaning Christmas was the star of the show. So, as a fan of the book, I asked Jared Padalecki, who plays Jack Stapleton and executive produced the project on the 2026 movie schedule with his wife, Genevieve Padalecki, why they did that. Here’s what he said:

When my wife and I, Genevieve, started shopping the book around after we met Katherine and got the rights to the book and to try and make it into a movie, Netflix had a great idea where they kind of said, ‘Hey, you know, there will be [a small number of] people that will watch a generic holiday movie, and [a much larger number of] people who will watch a Christmas movie,’ essentially.

Listen, while there are some great Thanksgiving movies, Christmas movies are talked about way more, and there’s a ton of buzz around them. Folks love the most wonderful time of the year, and Netflix figured more people would tune into Guarding Stars if it was centered around Christmas instead of another holiday.

So, with that in mind, the Padaleckis took that idea to The Bodyguard’s author, Katherine Center. She was all for the change, and simply requested that the spirit of the characters stayed the same. With that in mind, it didn’t really matter to Center where and when Guarding Stars took place, as the Supernatural star explained:

And so we talked with Katherine about it, and she's like, ‘I get it. Like, that makes sense. And I want as many people to become a part of The Bodyguard universe as possible.’ What was really important to her, that she stressed, was that the characters, like Jack and Hannah and the family, they kept their hearts. Ideally, you could put Jack and Hannah in Iowa in March, or you know, in Oregon in August, and still have the same thing happen. And so she was really receptive about, like, ‘Yeah, I understand. Like, people will watch.’

I think they accomplished this goal too. While Guarding Stars is quite different from its source material (the change at the end surprised me), Jack and Hannah, at their cores, are the same. The spirit of the book is 100% in the movie, and it feels very true to the source material in that way.

During our interview, Padalecki went on to praise Center’s open-mindedness toward the changes they made for this movie, saying some creatives can be “really precious” about those things. Overall, she got the vision of the film, and she was thrilled to see this world she created expanding:

But she's like, ‘Yeah, I get that.’ And I think she said it the best. She's like, ‘This isn't replacing The Bodyguard. This is expanding The Bodyguard universe of Jack and Hannah.’

So, that’s why Guarding Stars is a Christmas movie even though The Bodyguard has nothing to do with it. Moving it back to take place in December puts it in a category of films that people flock to at this time of year as they prepare for the holidays. No other holiday can do that at such a high level. So, this change does make a lot of sense.

Plus, who doesn’t want a bit of festive fun in their rom-com?

Now, with the Christmas season upon us, you can stream Guarding Stars with a Netflix subscription. And if you want to see Jack and Hannah over Thanksgiving, you can read The Bodyguard.

She graduated with her master’s degree in arts journalism and communications from the Newhouse School at Syracuse University. Since joining the CB team she has covered numerous TV shows and movies -- including her personal favorite shows Ted Lasso and The Marvelous Mrs. Maisel. She also has followed and consistently written about everything from Taylor Swift to Fire Country, and she's enjoyed every second of it.

6 Of The Comfiest Comfort Movies Of All Time Are Streaming In The Same Place

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I don't need the weather to be cold in order to feel the urge to cuddle up under a blanket and watch my favorite feel-good comfort movies... but it helps. With the hustle and bustle of the holiday season upon us, I'm keeping a close eye out for films that transport me right to my happy place, and it just so happens that there are several top-tier comfort movies that are all streaming in the same place.

Before we get into the movies, if you don't have a Peacock subscription, now's a really good time to consider signing up, as the streamer is running a Black Friday promotion for a limited time. You'll want a Peacock Premium subscription for the movies I'm about to talk about, as the Select plan, while significantly cheaper, does not include movies (it also excludes live programming and Peacock originals).

Get Peacock Premium with this deal for $8.99/month for the first 3 months, or $89.99 for the first year of an annual subscription. For the monthly option, the price will increase to its current cost of $12.99/month after the first three months are up (if you don't cancel before then).

An annual subscription for Peacock Premium normally costs $129.99, so $89.99 for the first year maths out to be the best bet, especially when taking into account that it breaks down to around $7.50 per month for 12 months. Be sure to read the fine print at Peacock, including that this deal is only good until November 30, 2026.

Ok, grab a blanket and settle in, because these comfort movies are all streaming on Peacock:

I'm going to sandwich this list with holiday movies, because 'tis the season, and love actually is all around. Richard Curtis's beloved and star-studded Christmas rom-com tells the story of several characters facing a variety of struggles with love. Between the romance, the humor and the magical UK backdrop during the holiday season, Love, Actually is non-stop good, cozy vibes from start to finish.

If rom-coms set in London (or Hugh Grant movies) are your thing, you can pair Love, Actually with a screening of Notting Hill. The extremely quotable 1999 rom-com stars Grant as a bookshop owner who meets and falls for a famous American actress (played by Julia Roberts). Its charming romance and delightful cast make Notting Hill the perfect movie to curl up and watch any time of the year.

As timeless classics go, The Princess Bride is among the elite. Blending fantasy with comedy and romance, the 1987 movie stars Cary Elwes and Robin Wright as the central characters of a story being read by a grandfather to his grandson. Not only is The Princess Bride one to watch (or rewatch over and over) with the whole family, it's an easy go-to comfort movie when you just feel like feeling all of the feelings.

I've mentioned a lot of rom-coms on this list, as the genre and comfy go hand in snuggly hand. Still, let's shift gears for a bit of romance in the form of a period drama. A good adaptation of a Jane Austen book might just be the gold standard there, and this one is particularly rewatchable. Peacock has Joe Wright's 2005 adaptation of Pride and Prejudice streaming. This is the version of the love story that stars Keira Knightley and Matthew Macfadyen as Elizabeth Bennet and Mr. Darcy, respectively.

Who doesn't adore a great second-chance romance story? Reese Witherspoon shines in this 2002 rom-com about a designer who returns to her southern hometown to finalize her divorce. While she and her ex (played by Josh Lucas) instantly butt heads, they also discover the spark that once seemed lost. If funny and romantic with a bit of Southern charm is what you're after, stream Sweet Home Alabama on Peacock right now.

I told you I was sandwiching this list with holiday movies, and here we are with a Christmas classic. I'm closing this out with one of my own personal favorites: Frank Capra's It's a Wonderful Life. I will never tire of revisiting this movie at least once a year to see George Bailey (James Stewart) discover the value of the life he's lived. While the overall message of the movie is inspirational, the way it's told -- George's guardian angel is tasked with saving him, but before he can do that, he's learning about George's life by visiting several pivotal moments -- that makes this movie such a great comfort watch.

Those are just a handful of movies you might be in need of if you're looking for a way to relax, snuggle in with a blanket and feel good. There's plenty of other content on Peacock too, including TV shows like The Office and its spinoff The Paper. You'll also find all eight original Harry Potter movies on Peacock (be sure to check out that deal I mentioned), as well as the original Hunger Games films (along with the prequel, The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes), and more.

There is an embarrassment of riches if you're looking to upgrade to a Wi-Fi 7 router for Black Friday

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Mesh routers are among the hottest-selling types of routers in networking, and leading the charge are products based on the Wi-Fi 7 standard. Mesh routers enable you to fully cover your home or office with wireless coverage that would otherwise be impossible with a single, standalone router.

Fortunately, numerous manufacturers are producing Wi-Fi 7 mesh routers, offering a wide range of options to choose from. If you prefer seamless integration with your Amazon account and Alexa services, consider opting for the Eero Pro 7. If you want endless configuration options and no-cost features, such as extensive Parental Controls, the Asus ZenWiFi BT4 has you covered. If you're looking for a delicate balance of performance and features at a bargain price, consider the TP-Link Deco BE95.

However, one thing to keep in mind when shopping for a network upgrade is that not all Wi-Fi 7 routers are created equal. While there are speed differences between products that depend on several factors (channel width, total number of streams, etc.), the primary differentiator among Wi-Fi 7 routers comes down to the number of supported bands. There are dual- and tri-band routers, with the latter offering the best potential performance. Dual-band routers only support the 2.4 GHz and 5 GHz bands, while tri-band routers support the higher-performing and shorter-range 6 GHz band. The maximum performance of the 6 GHz band is what gives tri-band Wi-Fi 7 routers a significant theoretical speed advantage over their Wi-Fi 6E predecessors.

Some manufacturers, such as Asus, also offer quad-band routers that feature either two 5 GHz wireless bands or two 6 GHz bands.

With that said, check out the extensive list below for all the best deals on Wi-Fi 7 routers from Asus, Eero, Netgear, MSI, and TP-Link.

The Asus ZenWiFi BD4 is an entry-level Wi-Fi 7 router and thus only supports two bands (2.4 GHz and 5 GHz). This 3-pack features up to 3.6 Gbps of combined throughput and covers 6,500 square feet.

The Deco BE23 is TP-Link's most entry-level Wi-Fi 7 router, sporting dual-band wireless, up to 3.6 Gbps throughput, and coverage of 4,500 square feet.

The Deco BE63 is a mid-range, tri-band Wi-Fi 7 router with four 2.5 GbE ports, 10 Gbps of combined throughput, and coverage of 5,800 square feet.

The TP-Link Deco 7 Pro BE10000 is a tri-band, Wi-Fi 7 mesh system that includes three wireless nodes. Each node comes with four 2.5 GbE ports, and the total wireless coverage is 7,600 square feet.

TP-Link's flagship Deco BE95 Wi-Fi 7 mesh router supports four bands, delivering up to 33 Gbps of throughput. It also features two 10 GbE ports per node.

The Eero Pro 7 is a mid-range, tri-band Wi-Fi 7 router that offers coverage of up to 4,000 square feet. Each wireless node features two 5 GbE ports.

The Eero Max 7 is Amazon's flagship Wi-Fi 7 router, featuring tri-band wireless, two 2.5 GbE ports, two 10 GbE ports, and up to 5,000 square feet of coverage.

The Orbi 770 is the latest in Netgear's long line of Orbi mesh routers. This one is a tri-band system with coverage of up to 5,500 square feet and 11 Gbps of combined throughput. You also get three 2.5 GbE ports and one 2.5 GbE port for WAN.

MSI's Roamii BE Lite is a newcomer on the Wi-Fi 7 mesh router scene, and it's a dual-band unit. Each node includes 2.5 GbE ports, and MSI even provides wall mounts in the box.

The MSI Roamii BE Pro is a high-powered, tri-band Wi-Fi 7 mesh router that won't break the bank. Each node includes four 2.5 GbE ports for connecting your wired devices, and the mesh system is controlled by a rather feature-packed smartphone app.

If you want a high-performance mesh router, look no further. The Asus ZenWiFi BE30000 is a quad-band solution, featuring three wireless nodes. Aggregate speeds across all four bands (you get two 6 GHz bands, one of which is dedicated to a wireless backhaul) are 30 Gbps, and you get coverage for up to 11,000 square feet.

GitLab: Director, Professional Services Technical Architects

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GitLab is the intelligent orchestration platform for DevSecOps. GitLab enables organizations to increase developer productivity, improve operational efficiency, reduce security and compliance risk, and accelerate digital transformation. More than 50 million registered users and more than 50% of the Fortune 100* trust GitLab to ship better, more secure software faster.

As the Director, Technical Architects on GitLab's AMER Professional Services team, you oversee one of the most technically credible and customer-facing groups in the organization. This team helps turn customer priorities into real adoption and measurable outcomes by guiding complex implementations, shaping new service patterns, and bringing emerging GitLab capabilities into production environments.

You'll own the Global Technical Architecture practice across both presales and delivery, managing a team of AMER Technical Architects who work on complex customer needs, early-stage offerings, and strategic engagements. This role is well suited for someone who can coach senior technical talent, bring structure to ambiguity, and keep the team focused on customer outcomes instead of task completion.

In your first year, you'll focus on stabilizing and growing the team, tightening the link between presales scoping and delivery, and turning field work into repeatable offerings. You'll work alongside architects in areas including AI, security, and broader platform architecture, while helping the team turn what they learn in the field into repeatable methods that can scale across Professional Services.

The Professional Services team helps customers adopt GitLab in ways that create lasting value. Within that organization, the Technical Architecture team handles some of the most complex and ambiguous work. This includes shaping early delivery approaches for new capabilities, contributing to high-impact customer engagements, and creating the patterns that help the wider organization scale successful services.

You'll manage a growing AMER team of Technical Architects, lead the global Technical Architecture practice, and work closely with Engagement Management, Professional Services Operations, Customer Experience Engineering, and other Professional Services colleagues. We play an important role in setting the technical standard for delivery while also helping define how new services should be scoped, delivered, and improved over time.

GitLab works in an all-remote, asynchronous environment built on transparency, iteration, collaboration, and empowered ownership. In our team, that means giving managers and architects the latitude to solve hard problems, exchange what they learn openly, and continuously improve how the practice runs. If you enjoy developing strong technical talent, creating clarity in fast-changing environments, and helping customers succeed through thoughtful consulting, you'll have a meaningful place to make an impact with us.

December full moon 2026: When, where and how to see the Cold Moon

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Your guide to December's full Cold Moon, from peak times to skywatching highlights.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The December full moon reaches peak illumination at 8:28 p.m. EST on Dec. 23 (0128 GMT on Dec. 24) as the lunar disk shines opposite the sun in Earth's sky. It is often called the "Cold Moon" in reference to the frigid temperatures that prevail in the Northern Hemisphere around the time of its rising, according to the Old Farmer's Almanac.

December's full moon will also occur close to perigee — the moon's closest point to Earth in its elliptical orbit — giving rise to a spectacular supermoon. A supermoon can appear 30% brighter and 14% larger than a full moon at its farthest point from Earth (also known as a micromoon)..

According to the late, great astronomer Fred Espenak, the Cold Moon will be just 221,667 miles (356,740 kilometers) from Earth when it turns full, making it the closest supermoon of 2026!

The full Cold Supermoon will make for a spectacular sight when it rises over the eastern horizon at sunset on Dec. 23, mere hours before reaching maximum illumination. Remember to use Time and Date's moonrise and moonset tracker for timings specific to your location.

Earth's satellite may appear larger than normal while close to the horizon. This is partly because it is near perigee, when it appears subtly larger in the night sky, but also because of a phenomenon called the moon illusion, which tricks us into perceiving the moon to be bigger than it actually is while in the presence of foreground objects.

The moon may also adopt a striking golden or orange hue around moonrise and moonset. When the moon is low on the horizon, its light travels through more of Earth's atmopshere, scattering shorter, bluer wavelengths of light and allowing longer, redder wavelengths to reach our eyes.

The table below details moonrise timings for a selection of cities spread across the globe for Dec. 23-24:

The December full moon is often called the Cold Moon in reference to the often freezing temperatures that can descend this time of the year. It's also known as the "Long Night Moon" by the Mohican tribe of North America, because of its proximity to the winter solstice, which marks the shortest day of the year in the Northern Hemisphere.

Old English peoples knew it as the "Moon Before Yule", according to Time and Date, while the Celts of Europe referred to it as the Oak Moon after traditions that centered around the hardy trees.

The December full moon also coincides with the Hindu festival of Datta Jayanti, when followers gather to celebrate the birth of Lord Dattatreya, who embodies the Hindu holy trinity of Lord Brahma, Vishnu and Shiva, according to the Government of Maharashtra. The December full moon also rises close to the Christian celebration of Christmas on Dec. 25, when followers celebrate the birth of Jesus Christ.

The Cold Moon will shine in the center of a majestic "Winter Hexagon" of stars bright enough to easily cut through the glare cast by the lunar disk after it rises on Nov. 24.

Begin by locating Sirius, the brightest star in our night sky, which rises into the southeastern sky shortly after 8 p.m. local time for viewers in New York. Next, trace an imaginary line to the blue supergiant star Rigel in the nearby constellation Orion, before hopping to the orange glow of Aldebaran, which represents an eye of the great bull depicted in the constellation Taurus.

Next we move to Capella, the jewel of the constellation Auriga, before jumping down to Pollux in the constellation Gemini and nearby Procyon, before closing the loop with Sirius once more.

Jupiter and the red light of Mars will shine low in the eastern sky in the hour before midnight, while Saturn will lurk close to the western horizon. Look to the southeast just before dawn to spot Venus appearing as a brilliant "Morning Star", with the red giant Arcturus shining higher in the predawn sky close to the constellation Bootes.

The December full moon presents a stunning opportunity to photograph the lunar disk as it shines in Earth's sky entirely free from shadow. Be sure to make use of our expert guide to photographing the moon, along with our picks of the best cameras and lenses for astrophotography, if you want to upgrade your equipment.

7:17 a.m. EST (1217 GMT) on Jan. 22, 2027

6:23 p.m. EST (2323 GMT) on Feb. 20, 2027

6:43 a.m. EST (1043 GMT) on March 22, 2027

These times mark the exact instant of full illumination. Your own moonrise or moonset may be hours earlier or later, depending on your location. For the best viewing plan, use an app such as Stellarium or SkySafari 7 Pro.

Liesman: Why Greenspan could bet on productivity — and Warsh needs to hike

📅 2026-11-25  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a discussion on CNBC's Halftime Report Wednesday, the question came up: Why can't Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh hold the line on interest rates the way his icon Alan Greenspan did at the beginning of the dot-com era?

Greenspan famously leaned against inflation hawks on his committee in 1995 and 1996, holding the line on interest rates amid strong growth, because he believed in the productivity promise of the internet revolution.

It would be nice if Warsh could follow the same playbook amid the artificial intelligence build-out and keep rates steady until the productivity arrives. Unfortunately, a quick look at the numbers shows just how different the situation is for Warsh when it comes to inflation, the magnitude of the capital-investment boom and the monetary and fiscal stimulus boosting the economy.

Bottom line: the Fed looks like it has to hike — and maybe substantially — to be anywhere close to the position where the Fed was in Greenspan's time at the dawn of a major technological revolution.

It may be that the massive capex spending on AI will mean more productivity and less inflation down the road. But the front-end build-out is proving to be more inflationary, and more challenging, to a Fed already dealing with high inflation. In many ways, that's the opposite of what Greenspan had to handle.

Fireworks AI Adds Tencent’s Hy4 Flash to Its Serverless API

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fireworks AI made Tencent’s open-weight Hy4 Flash available on its serverless API on November 25, 2026, pricing the Standard tier at $0.07 per million input tokens and $0.281 per million output tokens. The announcement adds benchmark tests that position the model as a low-cost option for professional and agentic work.

Cached input costs $0.003 per million tokens. A Priority tier is also available, and the service is served globally. Developers can instead use on-demand dedicated GPUs; the Hy4 Flash model page lists function calling as supported and fine-tuning and embeddings as unsupported.

Hy4 Flash uses a mixture-of-experts design, in which only about 12 billion of its roughly 220 billion parameters are active for each token. It accepts text and image input. The native context window is 1M tokens, while Fireworks is serving 512K at launch, a material constraint for applications built around very long documents or extended agent sessions.

Tencent introduced Hy4 Flash on November 16, 2026, as its most cost-effective Hy model for everyday productivity and released the weights on GitHub, Hugging Face, ModelScope, and AtomGit. Tencent says reinforcement learning reduced average end-to-end output tokens by 50%, while megakernels, 4-bit quantization, and DFly lifted decoding speed beyond 150 tokens per second.

Fireworks reports testing the model on seven professional benchmarks with the same harness used for other models on each leaderboard, at high reasoning. Costs are based on recorded tokens at Fireworks’ serverless prices, and the results are dated November 22, 2026. Hy4 Flash reached the score-cost Pareto frontier on every board where cost was recorded, meaning no listed model delivered a higher score at a lower cost.

Fireworks’ run of Epiq’s CorporateBench, which tests questions across thousands of internal company emails and meeting notes, produced a 75.9% score for Hy4 Flash: sixth of 14 models and slightly above Claude Opus 5.5’s 75.4%. The respective costs were $0.013 and $2.80 per task.

Rogo’s Big Finance Benchmark produced a 49.2% tie with Claude Opus 5 in Fireworks’ tests, at $0.0065 per task for Hy4 Flash versus $1.83 for Opus 5. Hy4 Flash also placed above Claude Opus 5.5 and Kimi K3.1 Pro, which each scored 48.2%. GPT-6 Luna was the only cheaper model on that board, but scored 35.3%.

On Sierra’s τ³-Banking agent benchmark, Fireworks reports a 49.2% score for Hy4 Flash against 49.1% for Claude Opus 5. The cost was $0.012 per trial versus $2.59. Across all boards where it recorded cost, Fireworks found Hy4 Flash was the lowest-cost model except on Big Finance.

Fireworks’ rankings were weaker elsewhere: Hy4 Flash placed 16th of 18 models on HealthBench Professional and 19th of 22 on DeepSWE. Tencent recommends the larger Hy4 for long-horizon software engineering because of its higher intelligence ceiling, reinforcing that the cost results do not establish uniform superiority across professional work.

Before opening the endpoint, Fireworks ran Hy4 Flash through its launch-validation suite on FireAttention, its inference engine, and compared the results with Tencent’s official endpoint. The company reports that every score came within one point of Tencent’s. The Fireworks results were:

The deployment’s appeal is therefore workload-specific: Fireworks’ tests pair near-parity with Claude Opus on selected professional tasks with much lower recorded costs, while the reduced hosted context and weaker health and software results set clear limits. Validation against Tencent’s endpoint gives developers a direct reference point for assessing whether Fireworks’ serving configuration preserves the model’s expected behavior.

Court upholds NYC rent freeze backed by Mayor Mamdani

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Manhattan judge dismissed landlords’ challenge to the Rent Guidelines Board decision to freeze rents on 1-year and 2-year stabilized renewals, a move Mayor Zohran Mamdani publicly supported. The court found a rational basis in the board record while criticizing campaign promises and a draft shared with a mayoral aide.  AI Summary

On Thanksgiving eve, Judge Brendan Lantry dismissed a landlords’ challenge to the Rent Guidelines Board’s late June decision to freeze rents. The ruling upholds the city’s first-ever freeze on both one- and two-year leases.

With the national real estate industry watching, the rent freeze became a major test of what a mayor can do to force affordability.

Owners of rent-stabilized buildings argued that Mamdani stacked the board with loyalists after promising a freeze, making the outcome predetermined. They also accused the board of following a campaign promise instead of the data on rising landlord costs. Lantry rejected both claims.

The ruling comes three weeks after an appeals court revived the city’s pied-à-terre tax rollout on second homes valued at $5 million or higher. Mamdani posted on social media that the city has billed owners of those homes more than $500 million.

That lawsuit ran parallel to the rent freeze lawsuit landlords filed in late July. It originally was in a Staten Island court but was moved to Manhattan in August at the city’s request.

The rent freeze took effect Oct. 1 while the lawsuit was pending and covers leases on more than a million apartments. The board had frozen one-year leases in 2015, 2016 and 2020.

Because the board acts in a quasi-legislative role, its orders stand if they have a rational basis, and courts may not substitute their own judgment, Lantry wrote. He found that the board’s 42-page explanatory statement showed it considered each factor the Administrative Code requires, including the industry’s economic condition and the cost of living.

Lantry said appointees who share the mayor’s views did not show collusion. He noted that “elections have consequences.”

But Lantry did not let Mamdani completely off the hook.

“Regarding Mayor Mamdani’s campaign for Mayor of New York City, the court would be remiss not to state that such comments, promises, and guarantees to freeze the rent were reckless and irresponsible,” he wrote. “The Mayor of the City of New York has no authority to unilaterally raise, ‘freeze’ or otherwise adjust the rent of stabilized units in this City – that responsibility is within the power of the Board and the Board alone.”

He called an email in which the board’s chair shared a draft of the explanatory statement with a mayoral aide before the vote “troubling.” He said it cast a “dark shadow” over the board’s independence. Without more, he wrote, the email did not justify overturning the decision under Article 78, the state law that lets courts review government agency actions.

The judge noted that petitioners agreed to end court-ordered discovery, which produced about 1,000 documents, and never sought depositions.

Lantry acknowledged the freeze could make it harder for owners to refinance or renovate. But he wrote that the legal question is not whether the policy is wise.
